## ENERGY OF NEW ACHIEVEMENTS

<!-- image -->

## ABOUT THE REPORT GRI 102-1 GRI 102-50 GRI 102-54

This  Integrated  Annual  Report  (the  'Annual  Report',  'Report')  was prepared  using  the  information  available  to  Public  Joint  Stock Company Rosseti Volga ('Rosseti Volga, PJSC', 'Rosseti Volga', the 'Company') at the time of its preparation, and includes an overview of operating results and key events for 2022.

The  Annual  Report  contains  information  on  the  Company's  goals and priorities, its performance in the reporting year as compared to previous  periods,  the  corporate  governance  and  risk  management systems, and its approaches to sustainability management, corporate and  social  responsibility.  The  data  provided  in  the  Annual  Report and specifically  the  financial  and  economic  performance  indicators calculated  on  the  basis  of  the  accounting  (financial)  statements prepared  in  accordance  with  the  Russian  Accounting  Standards (RAS),  include  information  about  the  activities  of  Rosseti  Volga. The data provided in the Annual Report containing the performance indicators from the consolidated financial statements prepared under IFRS, include information about the activities of Rosseti Volga Group of Companies. GRI 102-46

The  Report  was  prepared  in  accordance  with  the  Global  Reporting Initiative Guidelines (GRI Standards version), GRI G4 Electric Utilities Sector  Disclosures,  International  &lt;IR&gt;  Framework,  Stakeholders Engagement Standard (АА1000SES).

The Annual Report was prepared in accordance with:

-  Federal Law No. 208-FZ dated December 26, 1995 'On Joint Stock Companies' (hereinafter referred to as the Federal Law 'On Joint Stock Companies')
-  Bank of Russia Regulation No. 714-П 'On disclosure of information by issuers of issue-grade securities' dated March 27, 2020
-  recommendations of the Corporate Governance Code approved by the Board of Directors of the Bank of Russia on March 21, 2014 and recommended by the Bank of Russia Letter No. 06-52/2463 dated April 10, 2014 for the use by Joint Stock companies whose securities  are  admitted  to  organized  trading  (hereinafter,  the 'Corporate Governance Code of the Bank of Russia')
-  requirements of Rosseti Volga, PJSC to the accounting (financial) statements of the subsidiaries.

The Report also contains indicators of achievement of UN Sustainable Development Goals.

This Report is intended for a wide range of stakeholders.

## Key topic

The key topic of the Report is the 'Energy of new horizons'. Our goal is to show how Rosseti Volga implements the digital transformation program with absolutely new characteristics of reliability, efficiency, availability, controllability and customer centricity aimed at enhancing the service quality, mitigating the adverse effects on the environment and  health,  and  at  improving  the  quality  of  life  of  residents  in  the regions serviced by the Company.

## New information in the Report

The  Integrated  Annual  Report  contains  the  additional  information on  performance  of  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  in  the  area  of  sustainable development  and  social  responsibility.  The  sections  'Development Strategy', 'Business Model', 'Key Performance Indicators', 'Personnel  Management',  and  'Environmental  Protection'  were updated.  The  sustainable  development  report  contains  sections  on sustainable  development  management,  sustainable  development risk management and assessment, and information on ESG ratings.

## Disclaimer

This Annual Report contains forward-looking statements that reflect the  expectations  of  Rosseti  Volga's  management.  These  forwardlooking statements are not based on any actual circumstances and are not guarantees of future performance. The Company shall not assume any liability for any losses that may be incurred by individuals or legal entities acting in reliance on these forward-looking statements. Such forward-looking statements in each specific case constitute only one of the few possible case scenarios and shall not be interpreted as the most probable scenarios.

Some indicators specified in the tables, graphs and diagrams in this Annual Report may differ from the sum of summands as a result of rounding.

## ADDRESS OF THE CHAIRMAN OF THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS

GRI 102-26

## Dear shareholders, investors, and partners,

Due to consistent and effective solutions of the Board of Director and management team, the electric grid company achieved all target financial and business performance indicators and successfully handled major tasks under unfavorable economic conditions.

The Company committed to ensuring safe and uninterrupted operation of  the  grid  systems  in  seven  regions  of  the  Volga  Federal  District, implementing the advanced technologies, increasing the automation degree of power facility control, ensuring power grids consolidation, and enhancing service availability. The Company is an infrastructural basis for economic development of the regions within the Company's responsibility area that forms a reliable platform for further development of different business areas and high living standards.

The Company's high solvency, systemic importance and attractiveness for  the  investment  community  was  confirmed  by  AA  +  (RU)  credit rating  with  stable  rating  outlook  assigned  by  the  Analytical  Credit Rating Agency.

In  2022,  the  Company  pursued  the  innovative  development  and technological transformation of the electric grid complex, and active implementation of new R&amp;D projects that increase automation of the  operational  control  processes.  The  business  priority  plans  of the Company were based on the documents approved by the Board of Directors of the Company, including Plan of activities aimed to develop  the  production  assets  management  system  of  Rosseti Volga, PJSC for 2022-2024, and Road Map for the development of value-added (non-tariff) services of Rosseti Volga, PJSC. The above documents  established  ways  to  tackle  the  important  objectives for  enhancing  the  production  performance  and  developing  new business areas.

<!-- image -->

Last  year,  the  Company  had  to  deal  with  the  issues  of  primary importance  such  as  switching  to  the  use  of  domestic  equipment, components,  materials  and  software  for  the  electric  grid  complex in the shortest time possible. Ensuring trouble-free operation of the critical  information infrastructure and cybersecurity was one of the top priorities. The Company made a lot of progress in this area, and will pursue these efforts in 2023.

Key  objectives  of  the  Board  of  Directors  and  management  team involve increasing the efficiency of all business processes, reducing costs,  expanding  the  scope  of  services  and  service  offerings,  and balancing  interests  of  the  regions,  customers,  shareholders,  and investors. I am sure that clear strategy, high operational performance indicators,  significant  technological  and  professional  potential  is a  key  to  further  development  of  the  Company  as  an  advanced  and innovative electric grid company.

Best regards,

Aleksey Valerievich Molsky Chairman of the Board of Directors of Rosseti Volga, PJSC

## ADDRESS OF THE GENERAL DIRECTOR GRI 102-14

## Dear shareholders, investors, and partners,

In 2022, Rosseti Volga, PJSC pursued the Development Strategy of Rosseti Group for the period until 2030. The development of the Company's electric grid complex aimed at migrating to absolutely new characteristics of reliability, efficiency, availability, and controllability of the electric grids.

In 2022, a highly automated 110/10 kV substation, Sazanley, equipped with the intelligent process control system was put into operation in the Saratov region.

The present-day business environment is marked by the dynamic growth and  integration  of  smart  technologies  and  platform  solutions  into  key business aspects of the companies in order to boost the efficiency and build new business processes, products and services.

The digital transformation of the electric grid complex pushes on to a whole new  level  of  technological  control  in  the  power  sector,  and  gives  new opportunities  for  consumers.  The  implementation  of  high-tech  projects is key to switching to the electric grid complex control system based on integration of all processes and automated processing of bulk data.

In 2022, the Company maintained uninterrupted power supply to consumers of  the  Saratov,  Samara,  Penza,  Orenburg,  and  Ulyanovsk  regions,  the Republic of Mordovia and the Chuvash Republic, and achieved all target operational performance indicators.

The  Company  performed  a  full  package  of  activities  involving  technical maintenance  and  repair  of  electric  grid  facilities  without  any  delay,  that guaranteed the effective preparation for smooth and continuous operation during  the  autumn-winter  season.  To  that  effect,  the  Company  allocated 5,256 million rubles in 2022.

The  Company  brought  the  investment  program  into  action,  including reconstruction  and  technical  upgrading  of  the  electric  grid  facilities.  The program  budget  exceeded  9.6  billion  rubles.  Last  year,  the  capacity  of

<!-- image -->

174  MVA  was  put  into  operation.  The  most  significant  projects  included reconstruction of major feeding centers, Bezymyanka-3  110/10 kV substation and Pestravka 110/35/10 kV substation in the Samara region.

Enhancing the customer centricity is one of the top priorities of the Company. In  order to deal with consumers in the most effective way, the Company opened a 24/7 contact center and offered online services that become more and more popular. In 2022, the revenue from the expanding range of valueadded services increased by 47% as compared to the previous period. The Company also pursues the EV charging  station  program.  In  2022,  three charging stations of Mode 4 type (fast-charge) were put into operation in the Saratov and Samara regions.

In  general,  the  Company's  achievements  in  2022  showed  that  despite challenging  macroeconomic  conditions,  the  Company  is  able  to  follow its  strategic growth path and ensure reliable operation and technological development  of  the  electric  grid  complex  in  seven  regions  of  the  Volga Federal District.

Best regards,

Aleksandr Ilyich Gavrilov General Director of Rosseti Volga, PJSC

## COMPANY SNAPSHOT

## HISTORY OF THE COMPANY GRI 102-5

<!-- image -->

## JUNE 29

The Company was incorporated on June 29, 2007 in Saratov as a 100% subsidiary of RAO UES of Russia, JSC in accordance with the resolution of the sole founder of RAO UES of Russia, JSC (Order of the Management Board of RAO UES of Russia, JSC No. 191р dated June 22, 2007) as part of implementing the resolution of the Board of Directors of RAO UES of Russia, JSC on participation in IDGC (Minutes No. 250 dated April 27, 2007).

According  to  structure  of  IDGC  approved  by  the  resolution  of  the  Board  of  Directors  of  RAO  UES  of Russia, JSC (Minutes No. 250 dated April 27, 2007), IDGC of Volga, JSC was comprised of the following distribution grid companies (DGCs): Volzhskaya IDC, JSC; Mordovenergo, JSC; Penzaenergo, JSC; Orenburgenergo, JSC; and Chuvashenergo, JSC.

## JUNE 29 - OCTOBER 01

In the period from June 29, 2007 till March 31, 2008, IDGC of Volga, JSC supported operations of distribution grid companies affiliated with the Company according to structure of IDGC. As of October 01, 2007, IDGC of Volga, JSC operated as the sole executive body of the above entities.

## DECEMBER 25

On  December  25,  2007,  the  Management  Board  of  RAO  UES  of  Russia,  JSC  exercising  functions  of  the extraordinary General Meeting of Shareholders of IDGC of Volga, JSC (Minutes No. 1795пр/8 dated December 25, 2007) passed the resolution on the Company's restructuring through affiliation of: Volzhskaya IDC, JSC; Mordovenergo, JSC; Orenburgenergo, JSC; Penzaenergo, JSC; and Chuvashenergo, JSC.

## APRIL 01

On April 01, 2008, IDGC of Volga, JSC was finally restructured through affiliation of five distribution grid  companies: Volzhskaya  IDC,  JSC;  Mordovenergo,  JSC;  Orenburgenergo,  JSC;  Penzaenergo,  JSC; and Chuvashenergo, JSC. As of April 01, 2008, DGCs affiliated with IDGC of Volga, JSC ceased to operate as legal entities and started to operate as the Company's branches: Mordovenergo, Orenburgenergo, Penzaenergo,  Samara  Distribution  Grids,  Saratov  Distribution  Grids,  Ulyanovsk  Distribution Grids, and Chuvashenergo.

Since April 01, 2008 and till the present time, IDGC of Volga, JSC operates as a single operating company in 7 constituents of the Russian Federation: the Chuvash Republic, the Republic of Mordovia, the Orenburg region, the Penza region, the Saratov region, the Samara region, and the Ulyanovsk region; the Company's headquarters is located in Saratov.

## JUNE 16 - JUNE 29

As a result of entry into force of amendments made to the Civil Code of the Russian Federation (as amended on May 05, 2014) and abolishment of closed and open Joint Stock companies, the legal form of the Company was changed: Open Joint Stock Company Interregional Distribution Grid Company of  Volga  (IDGC  of  Volga,  JSC) was  renamed  to  Public  Joint  Stock  Company  Interregional Distribution Grid Company of Volga (IDGC of Volga, PJSC). A  restated version of the Articles of Association was approved on June 16, 2015 by the annual General Meeting of Shareholders and registered by Inter-District Inspectorate of the Federal Tax Service No. 19 for the Saratov region on June 29, 2015.

## JUNE

On June 2019, the Company joined the Unified Branding Standard of Rosseti, PJSC and operates under the brand name 'Rosseti Volga'.

## MAY 29

The annual General Meeting of Shareholders of IDGC of Volga, PJSC held on May 29, 2020 (Minutes No.  17/2020  dated  May  29,  2020)  passed  a  resolution  on  amending  the  Articles  of  Association  to reflect a change of the Company's name from Public Joint Stock Company Interregional Distribution Grid Company of Volga to Public Joint Stock Company Rosseti Volga. Amendments to the Articles of Association of the Company were registered by Inter-District Inspectorate of the Federal Tax Service No. 19 for the Saratov region on August 04, 2020. New full firm name of the Company in English: Public Joint Stock Company "Rosseti Volga".

## MARKET OVERVIEW AND COMPETITIVE LANDSCAPE

Service territory, presence on electric power transmission and technological connection market

Rosseti Volga, PJSC operates in the territory of the Russian Federation. Core business activities include  services  for  electric  power  transmission  and  technological  connection  of  power consuming devices (power installations) operated by legal entities and individuals to electrical networks. These operations are conducted in the territory of seven constituent entities of the Russian Federation: the Chuvash Republic, the Republic of Mordovia, Orenburg, Penza, Saratov, Samara and Ulyanovsk regions.

Major companies that carry out similar activities in the territory serviced by the Company, main competitors of the Company in this environment

Below is the list of major companies that carry out similar activities in the territory where Rosseti Volga, PJSC operates:

## the Orenburg region:

## MACROECONOMIC TRENDS OF THE REPORTING YEAR

The  economic  environment  in  the  reporting  period  was  marked  by  difficult  geopolitical conditions that resulted in the increased sanction pressure on the domestic financial and industrial system.

The Russian economy dynamics observed during this year was driven by 'prompt mitigation of financial stability risks' in spring, growing export and  decreasing  import,  'high  flexibility'  of  companies  from  different sectors, and significant increase in budget costs.

According  to  the  Russian  Statistics  Agency,  in  2022,  Russian  GDP decreased by 2.1% as compared to the previous year, that is lower than estimated by the Ministry of Economic Development of Russia (2.9%). Meanwhile, GDP in 'Electricity, gas and steam supplies; air-conditioning' segment was 0.1%.

In 2022, the Industrial Production Index decreased by 0.6% as compared to the previous year. However, the electric power, gas and steam supply sector increased by 0.1% on a year-on-year basis.

According  to  the  Russian  Statistics  Agency,  the  average  inflation  for 2022 amounted to 113.75% (2021) and 111.94% (by December 2021).

At the year-end, the price dynamics was better than estimated by the Ministry  of  Economic  Development  (13.9%  and  12.4%,  respectively) due to the following measures taken by the Central Bank of Russia: the regulator changed the key interest rate based on the external situation (on February 28, the Bank of Russia increased the rate from 9.5% to 20%, and on September 16, the rate was cut to 7.5%), and the stronger ruble decreased the import prices.

According to SO UES, the volume of electric power generated by power plants of RAO UES of Russia in 2022 totaled 1,121.6 billion kWh that is higher than the actual volume generated in 2021 by 7.05 billion kWh or 0.6%. In 2022, the power consumption amounted to 1,106.3 billion kWh that exceeded the actual consumption in 2021 by 15.86 billion kWh or 1.5%. In 2022, the net electricity supply to consumers at the boiler boundaries amounted to 47.92 billion kWh that is lower than the actual value in 2021 by 0.71 billion kWh or 1.5%.

The current geopolitical environment in the world, high sanction pressure imposed on Russia and specific sectors, high volatility of global prices for  energy  resources  makes  it  difficult  to  predict  the  short-term  and mid-term economic trends.

The  Company  operates  in  the  most  stable  sector  as  the  demand  for our  products  is  consistent  and  is  subject  to  minor  fluctuations,  and the risks associated with potential changes in prices for raw materials used  for  production  do  not  exist  as  the  raw  materials  are  not  used in  the  core  business  activity.  Despite  of  the  geopolitical  risks  and uncertain macroeconomic indicators, the Company will supply power to consumers without interruption and will pursue our efforts to ensure the environmental safety and built the infrastructural basis for economic development of the region.

## DEVELOPMENT STRATEGY

## STRATEGIC GOALS

<!-- image -->

The mission of Rosseti Volga, PJSC is to effectively use the distribution grid assets  of  the  Company,  to  maintain  their  reliability  and  to  attract  investment resources for the benefit of each shareholder and the Company in general.

As an integral part of the electric grid complex of the Russian Federation and Rosseti Group of Companies, the Company sticks to the Development Strategy of Rosseti, PJSC for the period up to 2030 adopted by the resolution of the Board of Directors of Rosseti, PJSC (Minutes No. 388 dated December 26, 2019).

## Strategic objectives of companies of Rosseti Group are:

<!-- image -->

ENSURING RELIABILITY AND QUALITY OF POWER SUPPLY AT THE TARGET LEVEL

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

INCREASE IN TOTAL SHAREHOLDER RETURN

ENSURING RESILIENCE TO CHANGES IN THE GLOBAL AND LOCAL ELECTRICITY MARKETS

To achieve the above goals, the Company will foster its core activities: digital transformation, development of new activities, further improvement of operational and investment efficiency, building of workforce capacity, etc.

## THE COMPANY SET THE FOLLOWING PRIORITIES BASED ON THE GOALS AND TASKS OF ROSSETI GROUP OF COMPANIES:

<!-- image -->

ENSURING SYSTEM RELIABILITY AND SAFETY to maintain stable operation of distribution grid complex in the region, safe operation of main and auxiliary equipment and facilities, prevention of hazards to life of population

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT OF QUALITY AND VOLUME of power transmission services, ensuring environment safety, formation of infrastructural basis for economic development of the region

GROWTH OF THE COMPANY VALUE that implies a continuous increase in revenues and profitability, expansion and qualitative renewal of the company's asset portfolio to satisfy the interests of the shareholders, to make Rosseti Volga, PJSC and its projects attractive to investors and to evaluate the efficiency of resource consumption and quality of managerial work.

<!-- image -->

In 2022, the Company demonstrated a high level of financial stability and  independence,  discharged  its  commitments  to  personnel  as defined  in  the  Collective  Agreement  and  Branch  Tariff  Agreement in  the  power  sector  of  the  Russian  Federation,  implemented  its maintenance and repair programs in full, and took measures aimed at reducing electric system losses.

At the year-end of 2022, the total effect of the power loss reduction campaign implemented across the Company amounted to 37.94 million kWh.

## Digitalization of integrated power grids is one of focal areas of the Company related to technological and innovation-driven development.

A  digital  network  is  a  highly  automated  network  that  enables visibility  and  controllability  due  to  digital  communication  systems and equipment supporting IEC protocols. Managed in real time, this network allows to track and trace parameters and operational modes of  all  participants  of  the  electricity  transmission  and  consumption process and provides self-diagnostics and self-restoration capabilities.

To maintain high level of reliability of the electric grid complex, new investments and inputs to the network infrastructure are required. In  order  to  make  these  investments  in  the  most  efficient  and effective  manner,  innovations  and  digitalization  processes  must  be implemented, with further reduction of expenditures for maintenance of  the  infrastructure  for  managing  technological  processes  and financial and economic operations of the Company.

Objectives of innovation-driven development and adoption of digitalization-oriented technologies will provide such benefits as reduction of OPEX and CAPEX, without increase in per-unit costs of the Company.

In order to achieve target levels for technological electricity losses and to reduce costs for compensation of electricity losses, the Company implements the Program of activities aimed to reduce electricity losses in the power grid of Rosseti Volga, PJSC. As part of digitalization of the power facilities, this Program provides for installation of multifunctional electricity meters for ingoing and outgoing 6-10 kV lines to/from 220/110/35/6-10 kV substations, and use of state-of-theart digital electricity meters at premises of customers on the retail electricity  market  in  accordance  with  delineation  of  balance  sheet attribution and operational responsibilities of the Company.

Benefits of implementing these activities will include the ability to  capture  reliable  data  in  real  time  to  calculate  electric  power balance  by  6-10  kV  busbar  sections,  to  calculate  grid  outputs and electricity losses for 6-10 kV networks, to obtain information about  network  technical  parameters  (such  as  current,  voltage, frequency, power factor, etc.), to improve its energy performance, and  to  reduce  consumption  of  power  resources  across  the integrated power grid.

In  order  to  enable  the  branches  of  the  Company  to  achieve  target levels  for  technological  electricity  losses  and  to  reduce  costs  for compensation  of  electricity  losses  according  to  the  RF  Electric Grid  Complex  Development Strategy (approved by Order of the RF Government No. 511-р dated April 03, 2013), the General Director of the Company prepared and adopted (by Order of Rosseti Volga, PJSC No. 60 dated January 24, 2023) the Program of activities aimed to reduce electricity losses in the power grid of IDGC of Volga, PJSC for 2023-2027.

<!-- image -->

Ensuring the quality of provided services through development of information space.

<!-- image -->

Improvement of the Company's internal operational efficiency and reaching break-even operation of the Company, including, but not limited to, activities set forth in the Program for Improving Operational Efficiency and Cost Reduction.

## Activity outcome in 2022

-  The average duration of breaks in power supplies to the consumers slightly increased to 1.37 has a result of adverse weather conditions. EU29
-  Electric power losses decreased by 0.01%. EU12
-  During the implementation of the investment program 1,756.86 kilometers of power transmission lines and 173.70 MVA of the transformer capacity were put into operation. GRI 203-1
-  The Company achieved the target key performance indicator 'Compliance with technological connection schedule'of 1.1 units.
-  Based on preparedness of the power sector entities to operate during the peak-load season monitored on the website of the Russian Ministry of Energy, the 'READY' index was assigned to Rosseti Volga, PJSC in compliance with the Rules of assessment of preparedness of the power sector entities to operate during the peak-load season (adopted by Governmental Decree of the Russian Federation No. 543 dated May 10, 2017). The Company was given a certificate of readiness to operate during the 2022/2023 heating season, and ensured the reliable and high quality power supplies to the consumers during the above period.
-  The profitability of the invested capital amounted to 0.7%.
-  In 2022, the Company reduced its operating costs by 7.6% (vs target value of 2.0%) as compared to 2021.
-  Earnings from operations (EBITDA) 1 for 2022 amounted to 6,778.7 million rubles with account of normalization factors under current economic conditions - 7,563.0 million rubles vs the target value of 7,540.2 million rubles.
-  Due to the measures aimed at developing the non-tariff services, the number of received requests for additional paid services increased by 99.56% on a year-on-year basis. In 2022, the revenue from non-tariff service sales amounted to 983,433 thousand rubles.
-  The amount of receivables for power transmission services increased by 14.34% as a result of the growth of the current receivables caused by upward changes made in the tariff solutions from December 01, 2022 and disputed receivables of Khimprom, PJSC and ENK-Sbyt, LLC incurred in 2022. During the reporting period, the Company increased the receivables turnover period from 31 days to 34 days.
-  In total, the receivables of the Company increased by 20%. During the reporting period, the Company increased the receivables turnover period from 41 days to 43 days.
-    Decrease in the level of occupational injuries occurred due to the fault of the Company's officials.
-  Zero group accidents and occupational diseases.
-  Labor efficiency increased by 3%.

## KEY RISKS

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

In order to improve the risk management system, Rosseti Group of Companies adopted (by Order No. 292 dated April 10, 2020) the risk management methodological documents, such as Risk Management  Regulation  of  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC,  ТП-МРСКВНД-892.**-**, governing the management of functional risks in  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  including  identification,  assessment, recording of information and development of risk management measures.

<!-- image -->

In  order  to  control  factors  of  formation  of  significant  and critical functional risks and mitigation of consequences of their occurrence, the Company adopted the Risk Management Plan of Rosseti Volga, PJSC for 2022 (Minutes of the Management Board No. 389/2021 dated October 08, 2021).

This  Risk  Management  Plan  of  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  for  2022 is  based on the Risk Reduction method, i.e., risk is controlled by reducing the probability of its occurrence and/or mitigating its negative consequences, if any, as the most acceptable risk management method considering the following principles:

-  maintaining balance of costs of implementation of the measures, their effects, and risk severity degree
-  taking into account opinions of experts of related functional areas, employees who are responsible for implementation of measures, and other stakeholders
-  reducing probability of occurrence of risk factors and/or their potential consequences

According to the Risk Management Regulation, the Management Board of the Company adopted the Risks Register of the Company for 2022 that contains 49 functional risks, i.e., aggregated risks of business processes and risks having significant impact on the Company's activities, in particular on key performance indicators of the sole executive body of the Company and thus on achievement of objectives of electric grid complex management and strategic goals of Rosseti Group of Companies.

-  implementation of measures shall give a specific and exact measurable result
-  measures shall be achievable from the perspective of available resources required to implement these measures
-  implementation  of  measures  shall  contribute  to  effective achievement of goals and objectives of the Company
-  to implement the measures, specific deadlines and responsible persons shall be defined.

The implementation of all planned measures having impact on factors  of  formation  of  significant  and  critical  functional  risks allowed to reduce the probability of risk occurrence and mitigate consequences of the risks occurred in 2022.

Information  on  functional  risk  management  with  materiality levels 'significant' and 'critical' is given in the table below.

The information security and cyberthreat risks at Rosseti Volga, PJSC include materialization of threats of violation of availability, integrity and confidentiality of the information processed by the Company.

Rosseti Volga, PJSC is an entity of the critical information infrastructure of the Russian Federation, personal data controller and entity of the State System of Detection, Prevention and Elimination of Consequences of Computer Attacks.

In order to take the aprior protection measures, at the present time Rosseti Volga, PJSC is implementing the investment projects aimed at building an integrated information security system of the electric grid complex (IISSEGC).

## Description of sustainable development risks

- 6.1.  The  risk  management  system  is  the  basis  for  strategic planning  and  implementation  of  measures  that  ensure  the Company's  sustainable  development.  The  risk  management system is integrated in all stages of the Company's activities including the process of making decisions on participation in the investment projects, budget setting, and development of strategic programs.

The management of ESG factors, i.e. factors associated with the  impact  of  the  Company's  activities  on  the  environment, climate, social sphere (including observance of human rights) and economy, along with risk assessment and management allows both to avoid negative consequences of occurrence of the  risks  related  to  the  human  capital  assets,  occupational health, safety and environment, and to take advantage of the opportunities associated with the above factors.

- 6.2.  Identification,  assessment  and  management  of  sustainable development  risks as part of arrangement  of the risk management and internal control system.

The Company's activities are exposed to external and internal risks. The timely identification, prediction and assessment of risks of different types is one of the most important objectives of Rosseti Volga, PJSC, one of the drivers of the Company's sustainable development.

The risk management system (RMS) is the basis for development and implementation of the Company's strategy that  covers  all  business  areas  including  the  sustainable development, and is an integral part of the decision-making system  at  all  management  levels  of  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC.

The  aposterior  measures  are  provided  by  the  plans  of  computer security  incident  response  and  elimination  of  computer  attack consequences  that  were  prepared  as  required  by  Order  of  the Federal Security Service of Russia No. 282 dated June 19, 2019 'On Approval of Procedure for Informing the Federal Security Service of Russia  about  Computer  Security  Incidents  and  Response  Thereto, Implementation of Measures Intended to Eliminate Consequences of Computer Attacks against Important Facilities of Critical Information Infrastructure of the Russian Federation'.

RMS key objective is to reach an optimal ratio between the preferred risk (risk appetite) and development strategy. The preferred risk shall be assessed at the stage of goal setting and strategic option review, as well as when developing the specific risk management mechanisms.

- 6.3. Description and assessment of the materiality level of physical risks and transitional climate-related risks.

In the regions covered by the Company's operations there is a risk of emergencies caused by natural disasters (storm winds, heavy rains, floods, including river flooding, snowy drifts, etc.) that may result in region-wide power outages and transport infrastructure (including electric transport) failure. According to  the  Company's  assessment,  risks  related  to  exposure  to natural hazards are minimal.

- 6.4.  In  order  to  mitigate  the  loss  risks  in  the  relationships  with suppliers  and  subcontractors,  the  Company  sticks  to  the ethic  business  practices  and  is  committed  to  encouraging the contacting parties to use this approach and contributing to their economic  development  by  taking  the  following measures:
-  support of small and medium-scale business and enterprises
-  anti-corruption activities
-  supporting fair competition.

As  no  loss  risks  in  the  relationships  with  suppliers  and subcontractors  have  been  realized  at  the  significant  level, the  probability  of  occurrence  of  the  above  risks  is,  in  the Company's opinion, very low.

At the same time, the volume of capacity for these indicators decreased as compared to 2021 (applications - 35%, concluded agreements - 21%, and implemented agreements - 9%). Such decrease is primarily due to changes in tariff rates for connection of applicants eligible for preferential tariff rates.

The variation of revenue from technological connection services for  the  period  2020-2022  was  caused  by  the  structure  and categorization  of  TC  agreements  with  due  dates  in  the  period under review.

The increase in revenue from technological connection services in 2022 (by 52% vs 2021) is caused, in particular, by an increase in the number of connections for applicants in the category 'up to  15  kW' who concluded agreements at the standardized rate or  the  rate  per  unit  of  maximum  capacity,  plus  the  increase  in the  number  of  registered  major  agreements  for  technological connection.

## Assessment of compliance with approved indicators of reliability and quality of provided services for the reporting 2022 year

The  planned  indicators  of  reliability  and  quality  of  services  provided by the Company's branches for 2022 are in line with long-term tariff regulations  adopted  by  the  regulatory  authorities  of  the  constituent entities of the Russian Federation before the end of the first long-term regulation period.

At the onset of each reporting period, actual quality indicators achieved by  the  Company  are  assessed  in  accordance  with  specific  nature of  regulations  adopted  by  the  regulatory  authorities.  The  planned indicators of quality of services provided by Rosseti Volga, PJSC in 2022 were achieved in full.

Marketing  strategy  for  promotion  of  additional  (non-tariff) services  in  the  market  of  power  grid  services  and  related markets:

-  publication of information on the Company's official website related to implementation and availability of additional (non-tariff) services, including electronic forms of request for such additional services
-  during  the  meetings  with  representatives  of  small  and  mediumsized enterprises, informing such representatives about the possibility of requesting such additional services
-  the  list  of  additional  services  provided  by  the  Company  is  made available in customer service offices
-  running an advertising campaign
-  proposals for the introduction of new types of services are prepared and worked on.

## Charging Infrastructure Development Program of Rosseti Volga, PJSC

As  part  of  implementing  the  Charging  Infrastructure  Development Program of Rosseti Volga, PJSC for the period until 2025, the priority task  defined  for  2022  is  to  commercialize  the  Company's  charging infrastructure. Commercialization of the charging infrastructure service provides for migration to a model for billing users for electric vehicle  charging  services  provided  at  special  EV  charging  stations. The  charging  infrastructure  will  be  developed  under  Rosseti-EV,  a unified brand of Rosseti, PJSC Group of Companies.

In 2022, interaction with Rosseti Tsifra, a unified operator acting as a unified center for selling charging infrastructure services of Rosseti, PJSC Group of Companies, was organized. The functional capabilities of the charging infrastructure management software were analyzed, and a list of the Company's charging stations for potential connection to  the  unified  software  was  made.  The  Company  prepared  a  draft agreement  for  connecting  the  Company's  EV  charging  stations

to  software  and  hardware  system  of  Rosseti-EV,  collecting  and provision  of  statistics  related  to  operation  of  EV  charging  stations, and accepting non-cash payments from third parties for EV charging services.

In  2022,  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  put  into  operation  three  EV  charging stations  of  Mode  4  type  ('fast  charging')  (Saratov  and  Samara regions),  including  software,  a  lighting  support  with  LED  lights,  a surveillance camera, and a sheltered area.

Besides,  four  design  specifications  for  facilities  covered  by  the Company's  investment  program  were  approved  in  2022  as  part of  the  project  'Creation  of  charging  infrastructure  in  the  territory of operation of Rosseti Volga, PJSC on the basis of the outdoor EV charging station operating in Mode 4'. It is planned to procure and put into operation four EV charging stations in 2023. Currently, design and survey activities for these sites are in progress.

According to the operating results for 2022, the revenue generated from sales of additional (non-tariff) services amounted to 983,433 thousand rubles vs 754,923 thousand rubles (target value), or 130% (in comparison with the previous year, the revenue in 2022 increased by 47%).

Having analyzed the operational performance of Rosseti Volga, PJSC in 2022 as related to the sale of additional (non-tariff) services, it may  be  reasonable  to  conclude  that  the  Company  demonstrated satisfactory results in this area, because the revenue from the sale of additional services in 2022 increased by 313,832 thousand rubles as compared to 2021.

In  order to reduce the rate of emergency shutdown of grid equipment and to enhance reliability of power supply for customers, activities performed under the Investment Program include reconstruction, modernization, technical refurbishment of power grid facilities and installation of reclosers on 6-10 kV power transmission lines. ## A set of measures taken by Rosseti Volga, PJSC during the preparation for operations of the integrated power grid during special periods

## Preparation for and operations during the autumn-winter season

Activities of Rosseti Volga, PJSC to prepare for accident-free operation during the 2022/2023 autumn-winter season were carried out on the basis of directives issued by the Ministry of Energy of the Russian Federation and Rosseti, PJSC, in accordance with the requirements of applicable regulatory and technical documents.

The  preparedness  of  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  to  operate  during  the 2022/2023 autumn-winter season was assessed by the Ministry of Energy of the Russian Federation. Based on assessment results as of November 25, 2022, the status 'Ready' with Readiness Index of 99 was assigned to Rosseti Volga, PJSC.

<!-- image -->

Installation of self-supporting insulated wires on 0.4-10 kV overhead lines

Based  on  the  resolution  of  the  Ministry  of  Energy  of  the  Russian Federation  (Order  No.  1185  dated  November  03,  2022),  Rosseti Volga, PJSC received a Certificate of Readiness to operate during the 2022/2023 heating season.

During  the  2022/2023  autumn-winter  season,  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC operated without any complaints, with Readiness Index being within the  permissible  values,  and  no  breaches  of  contractual  obligations were recorded.

Based on assessment results as of March 25, 2023, the status 'Ready' with Readiness Index of 99 was assigned to Rosseti Volga, PJSC.

Activities	 under	 the Innovation-Driven	Development	Program	planned	for	2022	were	implemented,	and	the	targets for	the	key	efficiency	indicators	of	the	Company's	innovative	activities	were	achieved.	The	key	to achieving the target values is to implement comprehensive projects for creation of intellectual substations,	 smart	 grid	 and	 automation	 of	 business	 processes,	 plus	 the	 development	 of	 new equipment	and	technology	solutions	as	part	of	R&amp;D	activities. GRI 103 -2

Deputy General Director, Chief Engineer K.G. Filippov

## INNOVATION-DRIVEN DEVELOPMENT

In  order  to  create  efficient  mechanisms  aimed  at  modernization  of  the  integrated  power grid, activation of scientific, technological and inventive activities, adaptation to innovations, improvement and development of the innovative system, the Company carries out innovative activities as one of its focal areas.

'Innovative activities' means a complex of scientific, technological, organizational, financial and commercial activities intended to implement innovative projects, create and support an innovative infrastructure.

## Innovation-Driven Development Program

The Company carries out its innovative activities in accordance with the Innovation-Driven Development Program (hereinafter, the 'Program'). The main objective of the Program is to ensure conditions for migration to the power grid of a new techno-economic paradigm with absolutely new characteristics of reliability, efficiency, availability, controllability and customer-centricity of the integrated power grid.

The  Innovation-Driven  Development  Program  for  2020-2024  and for the long-term period up to 2030 was approved by resolution of the Board of Directors of the Company on August 30, 2021 (Minutes No. 5 dated September 01, 2021) and put into effect by Order of the Company No. 784 dated September 03, 2021.

The activities under the Program scheduled for 2022 are outlined in  the  Medium-Term  Plan  of  Implementation  of  the  InnovationDriven  Development Program of Rosseti Volga, PJSC for 20222026  (hereinafter,  the  'MTP  IDDP')  approved  by  resolution  of the  Board  of  Directors  of  the  Company  on  September  08,  2022 (Minutes No. 6 dated September 09, 2022) and put into effect by Order  of  the  Company  No.  840  dated  September  16,  2022  'On putting  into  effect  of  the  Medium-Term  Plan  of  Implementation of the Innovation-Driven Development Program of Rosseti Volga, PJSC for 2022-2026'.

<!-- image -->

The defined key areas of the Company's  innovation-driven development make it possible to ensure technical and economic efficiency and operational reliability of the integrated power grid, reduce  the  human  impact  on  the  environment,  and  ensure  the Company's ability to compete in the medium and long term.

One of the key focuses of the Program is the implementation of research,  development,  testing  and  engineering  projects  (R&amp;D). The goal of R&amp;D activities is to perform technical, technological and  management  tasks  by  developing  innovative  concepts  and solutions  designed  to  boost  the  performance  of  the  distribution integrated power grid.

The  Technology  Register  for  key  focus  areas  of  innovationdriven  development  (adopted  as  a  guidance  by  Order  of  the Company  No.  62  dated  January  28,  2019  'On  adoption  of  TRIDGC-VND-755.01-18  document  as  a  guidance')  serves  as  the underlying  document  for  taking  innovation-driven  measures, including comprehensive projects and R&amp;D.

Activities under the Innovation-Driven Development Program are implemented within the framework of existing major programs of the Company, for which the volume of financing has been defined and  approved  by  the  Company's  Business  Plan.  Planning  of expenses for implementation of the Program is made on a regular basis,  and  the  demand for allocated resources is determined in accordance with the annually updated Medium-Term Plan.

## Transition to digital substations of various voltage classes - 35-110 (220) kV:

2.  Creation of an automated substation equipped with information and control systems able to
3. operate in a synchronized and coordinated manner, physically based at Yugo-Vostochnaya (South-Eastern) 110/10/6 kV substation operated by the Central Production Department of Orenburgenergo branch.

1.  Creation of a smart grid equipped with an intelligent control system based on the highly automated power distribution zones of Rosseti Volga branches.

## TECHNICAL POLICY

Rosseti Volga, PJSC implemented its key and most important investment projects using innovative, advanced and state-of-the-art technical solutions in accordance with Regulation of Rosseti, PJSC 'On the unified technical policy in the integrated power grid' (hereinafter, the 'Technical Policy'). The Technical Policy was approved by resolution of the Board of Directors of the Company (Minutes No. 1 dated July 06, 2022) and put into effect by Order of the Company No. 595 dated July 12, 2022 'On adoption of P-RV-VND-196.05-22 document 'Regulation of Rosseti, PJSC on the unified technical policy in the integrated power grid' as a guidance'.

## IMPORT SUBSTITUTION AND INTERACTIONS WITH EQUIPMENT MANUFACTURERS

In accordance with Order No. 278 dated April 06, 2020, Rosseti Volga, PJSC implements measures under the Corporate Import Substitution Plan of Rosseti, PJSC (hereinafter, the 'CISP') approved by Order of Rosseti, PJSC No. 46 dated February 05, 2020 'On approval of the Corporate Import Substitution Plan of Rosseti, PJSC'.

## In 2022, the following measures were taken as part of the CISP implementation:

1. Measures to improve procurement activities:
-  Approval/improvement of the procedure for mandatory analysis to  assess  the  possibility  of  replacing  imported  products  with domestic  products  with  equivalent  technical  characteristics and customer properties, when considering the procurement of imported products.
-  Approval/improvement of internal corporate technical and technological standards to ensure import substitution in engineering and production processes.
2. Incentive  measures  to  support  Russian  manufacturers  delivering products (works, services):
-  Generation of a list of imported products critical for engineering and production processes and having priority for the purposes of import substitution. Creation of a system for informing domestic manufacturers about the Company's plans to purchase imported products.
-  Participation  in  joint  projects  to  ensure  local  manufacturing of  products  comparable  to  imported  products,  or  projects  for setup/modernization of domestic production.
-  Generation  of  a  list  of  Russian  products  subject  to  validation, testing and pilot implementation.
-  Testing, validation and pilot implementation of domestic products at power grid facilities.
-  Financing of R&amp;D projects to ensure the development of importsubstituting products.
3. Achievement  of  performance  targets  of  the  CISP  for  imported equipment  procurement.  Targets  for  2022:  a  share  of  foreign equipment  in  the  total  volume  of  equipment  procurement:  target value - 4.8%, actual value - 3.7%.
4. Enhancing  cooperation  with  entities  of  the  Russian  Federation concerning  participation  of  regional  industrial  enterprises  in  the implementation  of  import  substitution  and  modernization  of  the domestic production facilities.
5. Enhancing cooperation with leading scientific institutions and centers (Skolkovo Institute of Science and Technology, Federal Test Center, PJSC, National Research Center Kurchatov Institute, National Research  University  Moscow  Power  Engineering  Institute.  AllRussian  Electrotechnical  Institute,  All-Russian  Scientific  Research and Development Cable Institute, etc.) in order to monitor advanced developments  having  no  comparable  counterparts  in  the  Russian Federation and abroad.

## TARIFF POLICY

## TARIFFS FOR ELECTRICITY TRANSMISSION SERVICES

As of January 01, 2018, all branches of Rosseti Volga, PJSC use a  tariff  regulation  method  known  as  long-term  indexation  of required gross revenue for 2018-2022 developed as per Order of the Federal Tariff Service of Russia No. 98-э dated February 17, 2012 'On approval of Methodology Guidelines for calculation of tariffs for electricity transmission services based on the method of  long-term  indexation  of  required  gross  revenue'  based  on the adopted long-term regulation parameters which may not be reviewed during the entire period.

Tariffs for electricity transmission services based on the method of  long-term  indexation  of  required  gross  revenue  (RGR)  are introduced  for  a  minimum  period  of  5  years.  These  tariffs  are defined year-by-year, for each year in the above long-term period, and may be updated each year as provided by this method.

Long-term  regulation  parameters  prescribed  by  the  regulatory authority  include:  basic  level  of  controllable  costs,  efficiency index  of  controllable  costs,  coefficient  of  their  elasticity  by  the number of assets, power losses, reliability and quality indicators.

capacity  within  which  the  grid  operator  supplies  power  to  its customers, etc.

Unlike the method of profitability of invested capital, the method of long-term indexation has limitations related to recordkeeping of  capital  expenditures.  Expenses related to financing of capital expenditures  out  of  profit  of  an  organization  which  uses  tariffs for electricity transmission services based on the method of longterm indexation of RGR may not exceed 12% of RGR.

During  the  long-term  regulation  period,  regulatory  authorities adjust  RGR  on  an  annual  basis,  taking  into  consideration  the difference between actual and target values of tariff calculation parameters,  including  changes  in  target  indicators  for  the  next periods.

These parameters and values are used by regional tariff regulation authorities for calculating RGR of a grid operator for each year in the long-term period.

Before  the  beginning  of  each  year  in  the  long-term  period, estimated parameter values for calculation of tariffs for electricity transmission services are defined, such as Consumer Price Index, the  amount  of  assets,  the  value  of  non-controllable  expenses,

The priority of the method of long-term indexation of RGR under economic stagnation is defined by the factor that the growth rate of  a  common  flat-rate  ('single  pot')  tariff  for  each  year  in  the first long-term period shall not exceed the level reflected in the forecast for the social and economic development of Russia.

The tariff setting undergo changes, with additions and clarifications being introduced almost every year. Below are key changes in the legislative framework adopted during 2022 and in the beginning of 2023:

<!-- image -->

Amendments were made to basic principles of pricing related to regulated prices (tariffs) in the electric power industry approved by Resolution of the RF Government No. 1178 dated December 29, 2011 'On pricing in the field of regulated prices (tariffs) in the electric power industry'. Pursuant to this Resolution, RGR of grid operators includes the estimated business profit that is used for calculation of prices (tariffs) for electricity transmission services, remains at the disposal of a grid operator and is disposed of at its sole discretion.

The estimated business profit is included in RGR in the amount of 5% of RGR of a grid operator for the next regulation period, taking into  account the expenses on payment for line losses for the next regulation period less the expenses on payment for electricity transmission services provided by other local grid operators. At the request of a grid operator, the estimated business profit can be set at a lower level.

<!-- image -->

Amendments were made to basic principles of pricing related to regulated prices (tariffs) in the electric power industry approved by Resolution of the RF Government No. 1178 dated December 29, 2011 'On pricing in the field of regulated prices (tariffs) in the electric power industry'. According to clause 87 of the Pricing Principles related to regulated prices (tariffs) in the electric power industry approved by Resolution of the RF Government No. 1178 dated December 29, 2011, the conditions for granting privileges to applicants requesting technological connection to power grids were changed. So, a preferential rate per 1 kW of the requested maximum capacity in relation to the entire set of measures for technological connection in the amount not more than 10,000 rubles per 1 kW (larger values are allowed as may be agreed by the federal executive authority responsible for state regulation of tariffs) and not less than 3,000 rubles per 1 kW (from July 01, 2022), 4,000 rubles per 1 kW (from July 01, 2023), and 5,000 rubles per 1 kW (from July 01, 2024) is set for a category of applicants (individuals, including with microgeneration) with the maximum capacity not exceeding 15 kW inclusive under the third category of reliability and at a voltage level of 0.4 kV and below, and provided that the distance from the boundary line of an applicant to the nearest power grid facility of the required voltage class as stated in the application does not exceed 300 meters in cities and towns and 500 meters in rural areas).

<!-- image -->

New Methodology Guidelines on determining charges for technological connection to power grids effective from September 02, 2022 were adopted by Order of the Federal Antimonopoly Service of Russia No. 490/22 dated June 30, 2022.

For branches of Rosseti Volga, PJSC, 2022 is the last year in the long-term regulation period (2018-2022) according to the method of long-term indexation of RGR.

In terms of power grid assets, branches of Rosseti Volga, PJSC hold a significant market share among all grid operators in the region. In 2022, the average market share of the Company in terms of assets, RGR and volume of electricity  transmission  services  for  ultimate customers was 44.8%, 63.1% and 73.3% respectively.

of  technological  connection  to  any  of  grid  operators  in  the  RF constituent entity. The applicable 'single pot' formation schemes vary each year depending on the level of adopted common ('single pot') tariffs, structure of local grid operators, and their applicable RGRs.

In  each  region,  grid  tariffs  are  defined  on  the  basis  of  specific tariff setting considerations, where a regulatory authority adopts a  region-specific  'single  pot'  scheme  for  determining  a  unified cost of electricity transmission services for customers regardless

The  tariffs  for  electricity  transmission  services  and  technological connection delivered by the Company in seven regions are set by a competent executive authority of the RF constituent entity in the domain of the state regulation of tariffs individually for each branch of the Company.

## Changes in revenue from all types of activity of Rosseti Volga, PJSC for 2020-2022, million rubles

<!-- image -->

As  compared  to  2021,  the  overall  revenue  of  Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC gained during the 2022 reporting year increased (+) by 1,801.5 million rubles (+2.8%), partly owing to the growth in revenue from electricity transmission  services  by  1,358.7  million  rubles  (+2.1%),  increase in  revenue from technological connection services by 128.9 million rubles  (+51.8%),  and  growth  in  revenue  from  other  activities  by 313.8 million rubles (+46.9%).

<!-- image -->

Key drivers affecting revenue from electricity transmission services in 2022 as compared to 2021 are:

-  a  1.5%  decrease  in  net  electricity  supply  to  service  users resulted in the reduction of revenue by 935.0 million rubles
-  a 3.6% growth in the average tariff for electricity transmission services resulted in the increase in revenue by 2,293.7 million rubles.

As  compared to 2021, key factors affecting  the  overall  RGR  in  the reporting period are:

-  increase in procurement costs to cover power losses - by 5%
-  increase in costs of services provided by FGC UES, PJSC - by 4%
-  increase in grid maintenance costs using own resources - by 8%
-  increase in costs of services provided by LGOs - by 4%.

In 2022, the share of the regulated segment in the overall revenue of the Company amounted to 98.5%, which is 0.5% below the level in the previous year.

Overall changes in the structure of RGR from electricity transmission services across the Company for 2020-2022 (tariff decisions), million rubles

<!-- image -->

Аccording  to  tariff  regulation  decisions  adopted  by  the  executive authorities  of  constituent  entities  of  the  Russian  Federation,  the overall  RGR  generated  from  electricity  transmission  services  of Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  in  2022  amounted  to  68,022  million  rubles, which is 2,546.6 million rubles (+3.9%) more than in 2021, including 29,386 million rubles for own needs, which is 2,167.4 million rubles (+8.0%) more than the figure reported for the previous year.

The  average  tariff  increase  across  the  Company's  branches  is backed by adopted tariff decisions, changes in the structure of net electricity supply by customer groups and by voltage levels.

New Methodology Guidelines on determining charges for technological connection to power grids approved by Order of the Federal Antimonopoly Service of Russia No. 490/22 dated June 30,

2022, became effective on September 02, 2022 and apply to charges for technological connection to local distribution grids.

For further details about standardized tariff rates, charge rates for a unit of maximum capacity, and preferential rates per 1 kW of requested maximum capacity for technological connection to the grids of Rosseti Volga, PJSC for 2022, please visit the Company's website (rossetivolga.ru), Section "Tariff decisions related to technological connection charges".

<!-- image -->

As  compared  to  2021,  a  68.2%  increase  in  the  average  rate  for technological  connection  to  the  Company's  grids  calculated  per capacity unit basing upon the actual revenue from this activity and connected capacity was observed in 2022.

In  2022,  the  actual  revenue  from  technological  connection  services  amounted  to 377.5 million rubles, which is 128.9 million rubles (+51.8%) more than in 2021.

The  growth  in  revenue  from  technological  connection  services in  2022  as  compared  to  the  previous  year  was  mostly  due  to  the increase in revenue for the following categories: other applicants for technological connection up to 15 kW - 167.0%, other applicants from 15 to 150 kW - 65.3%. In case of customized projects, the revenue demonstrated an exponential growth - by 24.9 times.

The actual volume of connected capacity requested by applicants in 2022 amounted to 605.6 MW, which is 65.3 MW (-9.7%) less than in 2021. The decrease in the maximum connected capacity in 2022 as compared to the previous year was mostly due to the decrease in the following categories: preferential applicants for technological connection up to 15 kW - by 5.3%, applicants from 150 to 670 kW - by 13.4%, customized projects - by 59.8%, and generation facilities - by 95.7%.

The actual average tariff rate for technological connection services of the Company in 2022 amounted to 623.3 rubles/kW and increased (+) by 252.8 rubles/kW (+68.2%) as compared to 2021.

This change in the average pay rate for technological connection in 2022 as compared to the previous year is due to both tariff decisions and changes in numbers and structure of the applicants requesting connection to power grids, technological connection of the applicants with  a  maximum  capacity  of  over  1.5  MW,  and  connection  of generation facilities.

During 2022, the executive authorities responsible for tariff regulation in the regions where the Company operates set the following charges for  technological  connection  for  four  customized  projects  for  the branches of Rosseti Volga, PJSC to the total amount of 17.94 million rubles, including region-wise projects:

-  in  the  Samara  region  -  one  project  (OOO  Energostandart)  to  the amount of 0.92 million rubles
-  in  the  Ulyanovsk  region  -  one  project  (OOO  Inza-Servis)  to  the amount of 0.62 million rubles
-  in  the  Republic  of  Mordovia  -  two  projects  (OOO  Ruzayevskie Pishchevye Tekhnologii, OOO EM-KAT) to the amount of 16.4 million rubles.

## SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT MANAGEMENT

Due to the Company's digital transformation program with absolutely new characteristics of reliability, efficiency, availability, controllability and customer-centricity of the integrated power grid, Rosseti Volga, PJSC implements the advanced solutions aimed at enhancing the service quality,  mitigating  the  adverse  effects  on  the  environment  and  health,  and  at  improving  the quality of life of residents in the regions serviced by the Company.

The sustainable development management system of Rosseti Volga, PJSC is integrated into the corporate governance system.

## THE GENERAL MEETING OF SHAREHOLDERS

distributes  powers  of  making  decisions  on  the  sustainable development issues between the management bodies of the Company.

## THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS

approves  the  development  strategy  and  focal  areas  of  the Company's  activities  including  the  sustainable  development, and performs the supervisory follow-up of activities to fulfill the  adopted  documents  and  achievement  of  the  sustainable development target indicators.

## THE COMMITTEES UNDER THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS

conduct the preliminary review of reports on implementation of  the  sustainable  development  programs  and  plans  of  the Company.

(MANAGEMENT BOARD AND GENERAL DIRECTOR)

## Social responsibility recognition

Due to the social responsibility development, Rosseti Volga, PJSC understands and recognizes the effect of their decisions and activities on the community and environment.

## Identification of and interaction with stakeholders

GRI 102-42 GRI 102-43

Rosseti Volga, PJSC understands the importance of taking into account the interests and expectation of internal and external stakeholders, and takes necessary measures to identify them. The behavior of Rosseti Volga, PJSC is based on such values as trustworthiness, justice and honesty. These values involve the concern for people, animals and environment, as well as the commitment to responding to any impact of the Company's operations and decision on interests of the stakeholders.

## SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT RISK MANAGEMENT AND ASSESSMENT

The sustainable development risk management is a part of the corporate risk management system of Rosseti Volga, PJSC.

Rosseti  Volga,  PJSC  considers  the  risk  management  as  one  of critical  elements  of  strategic  management  and  internal  control. The  Company's  risk  management  policy  includes  both  potential unfavorable events (threats) and favorable events (opportunities). The Company detects, evaluates and controls threats and opportunities on  a  regular  basis  and  adapts  its  activities  to  minimize  probability and potential consequences of these threats and to benefit from the opportunities, as well as submit this information to shareholders and other interested persons.

The risk management policy of Rosseti Volga, PJSC makes allowance both  for  shareholders'  interests  and  potential  consequences  of  its activities for other related parties.

The Risk Management Policy of the Company adopted by the decision of  the  Company's  Board  of  Directors  (Minutes  No.  26  dated  March 12,  2021)  is  an  organizational  and  legal  base  for  operating  the

Company's risk management system. The above documents contain basic principles of organization, implementation and control of risk management processes in the Company. The Policy outlines primary actions for building a risk management system.

Rosseti Volga, PJSC provides information on the risks  (including  the  sustainable  development risks) in the Issuer's Report as per Regulation of the Bank of Russia No. 714-П 'On Disclosure of  Information  by  Issuers  of  Issue-Grade  securities' dated March 27, 2020. Websites used by  the  Company  for  information  disclosure: http://www.rossetivolga.ru/, https://e-disclosure.ru/portal/company.aspx?id=12131

For  details  on  the  risk  list  see  the  section 'Key Risks.'

## SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY RATING

The social responsibility rating is based on the share of social costs (wages, taxes and charity expenses) in the company's revenue. In 2022, Rosseti Volga, PJSC was put on the list of companies under study for the first time. The share of social costs in the Company's revenue based on the results of 2021 amounted to 23.2% (the 8th place among the electric grid companies). https://akmrating.ru/reyting\_otvetstvennosty\_pered\_obshestvom/

The  social  performance  rating  has  been  calculated  by  AK&amp;M  agency  since  2018.  This  rating  shows  the  efficiency  of influence of the companies on the social environment and ecological system. In 2022, the social performance rating of Rosseti Volga, PJSC amounted to 3,271 thousand conv. rubles/conv. t according to the 2021 report (the 9th place among the electric grid companies). https://akmrating.ru/reytingsoceffektivnosty/

## HUMAN RESOURCES

<!-- image -->

In	present-day	conditions	of	dynamic	technology	development	and	process	automation, the improvement of professional competence of the personnel is of vital importance. GRI 103-2

Director for Personnel Management and Organizational Planning, Head of the Department of Personnel Management and Organizational Planning N.M. Kolesnikova

## RESULTS OF IMPLEMENTATION OF THE HUMAN RESOURCES POLICY

The key objectives of the human resources policy of the Company aimed to help attain targets of the Electric Grid Complex Development Strategy include:

-  planning  manpower  demands  to  ensure  availability  of  reliable information on operational and forecast quantitative and qualitative demand  for  manpower  resources  necessary  and  sufficient  for meeting the Company's goals
-  meeting the Company's demand for personnel with required skills and qualification in a timely manner
-  ensuring effective personnel performance
-  improving labor efficiency.

## Staffing level and structure

<!-- image -->

In 2022, the average headcount of the Company amounted to 20,364 persons, i.e. 2.1% less than in 2021. Personnel structure by categories: workers - 54.5%, specialists - 28.4%, managers - 17.2%.

-  organizational design
-  staffing and human resource development
-  personnel performance management (motivation)
-  social security and benefits
-  youth policy
-  occupational safety and labor culture.

<!-- image -->

The Company's employees have high professional skills

The Company's employees have high professional skills - over 86% of employees have vocational degrees, including 27 Candidates of Science and 2 Doctors of Science. The analysis of the personnel education level dynamics shows that every year the share of employees without vocational degrees goes down, and the number of employees with higher vocational degrees increases. Over the last 3 years, the share of employees without vocational degrees decreased from 15.1% (2020) to 14.0% (2022) (reduction by 1.1%) and the share of employees with higher vocational degrees increased by 0,7%.

## PERSONNEL TRAINING AND DEVELOPMENT GRI 404-2

Staff training is one of the top-priority areas of the Human Resources and Social Policy of the Company and is governed by the Rules for Work with Personnel of Energy Companies of  the  Russian  Federation,  Regulations  for  Training  and  Advance  Training  of  Personnel  of the  Company,  and  other  regulatory  documents  related  to  training  of  personnel  of  energy companies

During the reporting year, off-site training events were attended by 18,988 employees (93.2% of the average headcount), which complies with the key target of the Human Resources and Social Policy of the Company (30%) and is reduced by 3.5% as compared to 2021 (96.7%).

Operational  staff  forms  the  largest  share  of  trained  employees  96.3%, or 18,287 employees. In 2021, this value amounted to 86.3%.

In  2022,  16,182  employees,  or  85.2%  of  the  total  number  of  all trained employees completed vocational training, recurrent training and  advanced  vocational  training  courses  in  the  corporate  training centers. In 2021, this value was 89.1% of the total number of trained employees.

## EMPLOYEE REMUNERATION SYSTEM

The Company adopted the time-plus-bonus compensation system. Pursuant to the applicable labor  law  of  the  Russian  Federation  and  employment  agreements  concluded  between  the employees  and  employer,  the  relationship  as  related  to  labor  remuneration  between  the Employer and Employees who are in the employment relationship with the Employer shall be governed by the Regulations on Compensation and Monetary Incentive.

The compensation system adopted by the Company that promotes the strategic goals of the Company is based on the following principles:

<!-- image -->

Equal pay for work of equal value that depends on employee's qualification, complexity of works, labor input and quality

<!-- image -->

Differentiation of compensation depending on labor intensity (work load), responsibility, powers, subordination, managerial and professional qualities

<!-- image -->

Transparent and objective performance assessment system

The variable salary component depends on the key performance indicators (KPIs) aimed at achieving the strategic priorities set by the Electric Grid Complex Development Strategy. The KPIs system for managers and employees that relies in their routine functions, requirements to final work results, effect on achievement of the final work results is implemented all the way to power distribution zones. The KPIs system is regularly revised basing on the results of the effectiveness analysis. The list of KPIs for the General Director of the Company is shown in the section 'Key Performance Indicators.'

and  Mordovian  State  University  named  after  N.P.  Ogarev,  Togliatti State  University,  Penza  State  University,  Penza  State  Technological University, Ulyanovsk State Technical University, and Ulyanovsk State University.

In  addition  to  agreements  with  flagship  universities,  branches  of the  Company  also  conduct  vocational  guidance  among  students of  universities  and  colleges  in  the  regions  where  the  Company  is present including Chuvash State University named after I.N. Ulyanov, Cheboksary Technical School of Construction and Municipal Economy, Cheboksary Electromechanical College, Saransk Energy and Electronic Engineering Technical School named  after A.I. Polezhaev, and Industrial and Economic College in the city of Engels. The Company conducts traditional Doors Open Days for students of universities and colleges  involving  demonstration  exercises  at  training  grounds  and tours around the production facilities to give them opportunity to learn more about the Company's activities.

## YOUTH POLICY

In  order  to  attract  young  professionals,  the  Company  implements  a  systematic  youth  policy which involves vocational counseling and guidance for schoolchildren, participation in practical training of specialists, vocational adaptation and development of young specialists.

## Career guidance activities

The Company is engaged in preparation for and conducting of Rosseti's All-Russia Olympiad  of Schoolchildren. In 2022, the  Olympiad attracted  660  schoolchildren  who  arrived  from  7  regions  serviced by the Company, and among them 8 schoolchildren became winners and awardees, and 5 schoolchildren participated in the Power Project Session at Orlyonok All-Russian Child Center.

The  above  children-oriented  projects  are  intended  to  spark  their interest  in  the  electric  power  industry  and  to  select  the  most enthusiastic candidatures for sponsored industry-specific education at  the  higher  educational  institutions  and  secondary  specialized educational institutions.

Pursuing own benefit, the Company provides training opportunities at  the  higher  educational  institutions  and  secondary  specialized educational institutions for 33 students, and among them 19 students entered universities and colleges in 2022.

To  provide  practical  training  opportunities  for  students,  in  2022, the Company organized internships for 621 students of educational organizations, including 108 students who undertook an internship on a payment basis. 485 students (78% of the total number of trainees) pursue  industry-oriented  studies.  25  students  take  training  under the  employer-sponsored  education  contracts  for  the  benefit  of  the Company.

## Work with young professionals of the Company

In  order  to  involve  more  young  people  in  public,  socially  significant  and  recreational  activities  and  to  promote  leadership  among  young specialists, the branches of the Company formed the Youth Councils coordinating and uniting young employees under 35 years.

## THE MAIN ACTIVITIES OF THE YOUTH COUNCILS INCLUDE:

-  creation of the environment for personal fulfillment, adaptation and consolidation of young employees
-  interaction of youth representatives with the union organizations and Council of Veterans
-  fulfillment of the creative potential of young people by giving them the opportunity to take part in contests and festivals
-  healthy lifestyle promotion and arrangement of sport events for young people.

In accordance with the Regulation for Organization of Student Teams Activities at the power grid facilities, in 2022, the Company organized 12  student  teams  that  included  185  students  from  20  educational institutions  in  order  to  foster  practical  training  of  students  who pursue energy studies, encourage them to seek job opportunities in the power grid complex, and revive traditions of labor education of young people.

The students who had necessary qualification and electrical safety access  level  were  engaged  in  auxiliary  and  repair  works  at  the Company's facilities.

The  EnergoStart  team  from  Orenburgenergo  took  part  in  creative competitions and was awarded diplomas of 2nd degree in 2 categories of  the  photography  competition,  video  contest  and  art  helmet competition. The performance staged by the above team jointly with volunteers of the Youth Council of the branch was named one of top five performances and presented at the solemn closure ceremony of all-Russian season of the student teams of Rosseti Group in Moscow.

In  2022,  the  Company  employed  116  graduates  from  universities and colleges including 17 students who undertook internship in the Company.

The  young  specialists  enlisted  in  the  Company's  talent  pools  take  part  in  training  programs,  industry-specific  national  and regional conferences providing opportunities for attendees from different electric grid companies to network and share best practices with their colleagues, to adopt new technologies and to introduce more efficient operating procedures.
