## Criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle

1.  At least one third of members of the Board of Directors shall be independent directors.

1.  In the reporting period, the independent directors (who have no conflict of interests) pre-assessed the significant corporate actions associated with potential conflict of interest and submitted the results of such assessment to the Board of Directors.

## 2 .5 The Chairperson of the Board of Directors contributes to discharging duties of the Board of Directors in the most effective way.

- 2.5.1 The Chairperson of the Board of Directors is an independent director or senior independent director selected among the elected independent directors who is in charge of coordination of activities of the independent directors and interaction with the Chairperson of the Board of Directors.
1.  The Chairperson of the Board of Directors is an independent director or senior independent director selected among the elected independent directors.
2. The role, rights and obligations of the Chairperson of the Board of Directors (or senior independent director, if applicable) are properly determined in the internal documents of the Company.

Partially non-observed.  The number of independent members of the Board of Directors of the Company in the reporting period was less than 1/3 of the size of the Board of Directors of the Company which is 11 members.  To meet this recommendation of the Code, the number of independent members of the Board of Directors of the Company shall be more than three (at least four) members.  The Board of Directors of the Company has two independent directors in compliance with the MICEX Listing Regulations, according to which at least two independent directors shall be present.  Despite the Company's commitment to comply with this recommendation, the Management of the Company have a limited influence on the election of the Board of Directors of the Company as members to the Board of Directors of the Company shall be nominated and elected by the shareholders.  Risks associated with partial observance of the recommendations of the Code are mitigated due to effective management of activities of the Company's independent directors.  The information on a planned increase in number of nominated independent directors is not available, thus this increase in not expected.  ## observed partially observed  -  non-observed  ## 1.  Non-observed.  In the reporting period, the independent directors of the Company did not submit any report on the assessment of significant corporate actions connected with potential conflict of interests to the Board of Directors.  The above recommendations are not implemented, as no such procedures are provided by the Company's internal documents.  Seeking to implement this recommendation, the Company is intended to prepare draft amendments and additions to the Company's internal documents in the next reporting periods with the account of the need to formalize the procedure for assessing the significant corporate actions associated with potential conflict of interests.  ## observed  -  partially observed non-observed  ## 1.  Non-observed.  The Chairperson of the Board of Directors is not an independent director. The issue on election of the Senior Independent Director may be decided on the initiative of the independent directors after the annual General Meeting of Shareholders and once new members of the Board of Directors are elected.  2. Newly elected members of  the Board of Directors are provided sufficient information about the  Company and work of the Board of Directors within the shortest time  possible.  1.  According to the internal documents of the Company, members of  the Board of Directors are entitled to obtain access to information  and documents regarding the Company and its subsidiaries they  need to discharge their duties as members of the Board of Directors  of the Company, and the executive bodies of the Company must  provide such information and documents.  2. The Company implements a standardized program of familiarizing  activities for newly elected members of the Board of Directors.   |

| ## No. Principles of corporate governance  ## Criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle  ## 2 .7 Meetings of the Board of Directors, preparation for the meetings and participation of members of the Board of Directors in the meetings ensure the effective operations of the Board of Directors.  2.7.1  Meetings of the Board of Directors are held whenever necessary  considering the scale of activities and current objectives of the  Company.  1. The Articles of Association of the Company prescribe that  decisions on the most important matters, including issues listed  in Recommendation 170 of the Code, must be made at meetings of  the Board of Directors by the qualified majority of at least 3/4 of  the votes or by the majority of votes of all members of the Board  of Directors.  ## 2 .8 The Board of Directors forms committees for preliminary consideration of the most vital issues related to the Company's operations.  - 2.8.1 To preliminarily consider issues relating to control of the Company's financial and economic operations, the Audit Committee consisting of independent directors was formed.  1.  The Board of Directors formed the Audit Committee consisting of  solely independent directors.  2. The internal documents of the Company determine the tasks of the  Audit Committee, including the tasks set out in Recommendation  172 of the Code.  3. At least one member of the Audit Committee, being an  independent director, possesses experience and knowledge of  preparation, analysis and assessment of accounting (financial)  statements.  4. Meetings of the Audit Committee were held at least once per quarter during the reporting period.   |

The Regulation on the Personnel and Awards Committee under the Board of Directors of Rosseti Volga, PJSC sets out tasks of the Committee, including tasks outlined in Recommendation 180 of the Code. However, this version of the document was approved in 2014, and hence it does not contain all norms listed in the Recommendation and conditions (events) when the Personnel and Awards Committee shall consider the issue on revision of the Company's policy on remuneration of members of the Board of Directors of the Company, executive bodies and other key management personnel of the Company.

The Corporate Governance Code of the Bank of Russia does not contain any recommendations regarding inclusion of conditions (events) when the Awards Committee shall consider the issue on revision of the Company's policy on remuneration of members of the Board of Directors, executive bodies and other key management personnel in the internal documents of a public Joint Stock company.

To date, the regulator has not issued any clarifications regarding specific conditions (events) that shall be captured in the internal documents, as well as any definition procedure, and this significantly complicates the observance of the above criterion.

The Company plans to make necessary amendments to the internal documents once the above clarifications are issued.

The Board of Directors of Rosseti Volga, PJSC formed the Personnel and Awards Committee under the Board of Directors of Rosseti Volga, PJSC responsible for dealing with tasks of the Nominations (Appointments, Personnel) Committee set forth by Recommendation 186 of the Code.

Explanations of deviations from criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle

## No. Principles of corporate governance

Criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle

## 7 .1 Any actions that will or may significantly affect the structure of the Company's authorized capital and its financial position and, accordingly, the position of its shareholders (significant corporate actions) should be taken on fair terms and conditions ensuring that the rights and interests of the shareholders as well as other stakeholders are observed.

- 7.1.1 Significant corporate actions shall be deemed to include reorganization of the Company, acquisition of 30 or more percent of its voting shares (takeover), settling any significant transactions by the Company, increasing or decreasing the Company's authorized capital, listing and delisting of its shares, as well as other actions which might result in significant changes in rights of its shareholders or violation of their interests. The Articles of Association of the Company contain a list (criteria) of transactions or other actions classified as significant corporate actions, and such actions fall within the competence of the Board of Directors of the Company.

1.  The Articles of Association of the Company contain a list (criteria) of transactions or other actions classified as significant corporate actions. According to the Articles of Association of the Company, the decision-making in respect of significant corporate actions falls within the competence of the Board of Directors. If the law explicitly attributes significant corporate actions to the area of competence of the General Meeting of Shareholders, the Board of Directors provides appropriate recommendations to the shareholders.

<!-- image -->

Status of compliance with the corporate governance principle observed

-  partially observed non-observed

Explanations of deviations from criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle

## 1.  Partially observed.

The Articles of Association of the Company do not contain any definition of significant corporate actions.

According to the Corporate Governance Code, the significant corporate actions shall be deemed to include reorganization of the Company, acquisition of 30 or more percent of its voting shares (takeover), settling any significant transactions by the Company, increasing or decreasing the Company's authorized capital, listing and delisting of its shares, as well as other actions which might result in significant changes in rights of its shareholders or violation of their interests.

According to the Articles of Association of the Company, the competence of the Board of Directors and General Meeting of Shareholders covers a great number of issues which relate, with account of Recommendation 303 of the Code, to the significant corporate actions:

-  reorganization of the Company (sub-clause 2, clause 10.2, Article 10)
-  increasing/decreasing the Company's authorized capital (sub-clauses 5 and 6, clause 10.2, Article 10)
-  splitting and consolidation of the Company's shares (sub-clause 7, clause 10.2, Article 10)
-  making decisions on participation in financial and industrial groups, associations and other unions of profit-making organizations (subclause 18, clause 10.2, Article 10)
-  making decisions on requesting to exclude the Company's shares and/ or issuable securities convertible into its shares from the quotation list (sub-clause 22, clause 10.2, Article 10)
-  requesting to include the Company's shares and/or issuable securities convertible into its shares in the quotation list (sub-clause 54, clause 15.1, Article 15).

According to the Corporate Governance Code, the significant transactions of the Company involve major transactions of the Company, related party transactions of high significance for the Company (the level of significance shall be defined by the Company), as well as other transactions recognized by the Company as significant transactions.

Pursuant to the provisions of Federal Law No. 208-FZ dated December 26, 1995 'On Joint Stock Companies', the procedure for approval of major transactions and related party transactions shall be defined by the Articles of Association of the Company.

## No. Principles of corporate governance

- 7.1.2 The Board of Directors plays a key role in passing resolutions or making recommendations relating to significant corporate actions; for that purpose, it relies on opinions of the Company's independent directors.
- 7.1.3 When taking any significant corporate actions which would affect rights or legitimate interests of the Company's shareholders, equal terms and conditions should be ensured for all the shareholders; if statutory mechanisms designed to protect the shareholder rights prove to be insufficient for that purpose, additional measures should be taken with a view to protecting the rights and legitimate interests of the Company's shareholders. Herewith, the Company is based not only on formal requirements of the law, but also on the corporate governance principles set out in the Code.

<!-- image -->

Criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle

1.  The Company has a procedure prescribing that the independent directors shall declare their opinion in relation to significant corporate actions prior to their approval.
1.  According to the Articles of Association of the Company, the approval of other transactions of significant importance for the Company (in addition to transactions provided by the law) shall pertain to the competence of the Board of Directors taking into account the nature of the Company's operations.
2. During the reporting period, all significant corporate actions were subject to pre-approval before their execution.

Status of compliance with the corporate governance principle

Explanations of deviations from criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle

The Articles of Association of the Company also define transactions that may be settled after pre-approval by the Board of Directors:

-  transactions associated with free transfer of the Company's property or property rights (claims) to the Company or third party; transactions associated with release from property liability to the Company or third party; transactions associated with delivery of free services (works) by the Company to third parties in cases (amounts) determined by the Board of Directors of the Company in individual resolutions, and making decisions on settlement of these transactions by the Company if the above cases (amounts) were not defined (sub-clause 30, clause 15.1, Article 15)
-  transactions involving non-current assets of the Company in the amount exceeding 10 percent of the book value of these assets of the Company according to the accounting (financial) statements as of the previous reporting date; transactions (including several related transactions) associated with acquisition, disposal or possibility to dispose property constituting fixed assets, intangible assets, construction-in-progress facilities intended for production, transfer, dispatching and distribution of electric power and heat in cases (amounts) determined by the Board of Directors of the Company in individual resolutions; or if these cases (amounts) were not defined by the Board of Directors of the Company, transactions for a period of over 5 years related to assignment of immovable property and power grid facilities for temporary possession and use or for temporary use, or acquisition of immovable property for temporary possession and use or for temporary use, in cases (amounts) as may be individually defined by the Board of Directors of the Company or if such cases (amounts) are not defined by the Board of Directors of the Company (sub-clause 38, clause 15.1, Article 15)
-  transactions that might result in commitments expressed in a foreign currency (or commitments attached to a foreign currency), transactions with derivative financial instruments in cases and in the amounts determined by the Board of Directors of the Company in individual resolutions, or defining the Company's policy related to transactions with derivative financial instruments if such cases (amounts) are not determined by the Board of Directors of the Company (sub-clause 49, clause 15.1, Article 15).

When the issues on taking/approval of significant corporate actions are referred for consideration by the General Meeting of Shareholders, the Board of Directors of the Company shall provide appropriate recommendations to the shareholders.

Taking the foregoing into consideration, the Company is not going to make any amendments to the internal documents in the short term.

| ## No. Principles of corporate governance  ## Criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle  Status of compliance with the corporate governance principle  Explanations of deviations from criteria for assessing compliance with the corporate governance principle  ## 7 .2 The Company has in place a procedure for taking any significant corporate actions that would enable its shareholders to receive full information on such actions in due time and influence them, and that would also guarantee that the shareholder rights are observed and duly protected in the course of taking such actions.  - 7.2.1 Information on significant corporate actions is disclosed with explanations of reasons, conditions and consequences of such actions.  1.  If the Company performed any significant corporate actions in the reporting period, the Company timely disclosed the detailed information on such actions, including reasons, conditions and consequences of such actions for shareholders.  -  observed partially observed  non-observed  - 7.2.2 Rules and procedures related to significant corporate actions are specified in the internal documents of the Company.  1.  The Company's internal documents define in which case and how an independent appraiser may be engaged to determine the value of the assets disposed of or purchased under a major transaction or a related party transaction.  2. The Company's internal documents prescribe engagement of an independent appraiser to determine the value of the Company's shares being purchased or sold.  3. The internal documents of the Company contain an extended list of criteria for recognition of members of the Board of Directors or other legally defined persons as parties interested in the Company's transactions.  observed  -  partially observed non-observed  1.  Observed.  2. Observed.  3. Non-observed.  The internal documents of the Company do not prescribe to deter any member of the Board of Directors, sole executive body, collective executive body of the Company or its controlling entity, or a person empowered to give binding instructions to the Company who has no formal interest in the Company's transactions but who has any conflict of interest or other actual interest from voting on the issue on approval of such transaction.  The companies of Rosseti Group conclude a great number of related party transactions.  At the present time, the legislation tends to simplify the procedure for settling related party transactions by companies and reduce the total number of such transactions. For example, starting from January 01, 2017, Federal Law No. 343-FZ dated July 03, 2016 introduced a new procedure for settling related party transactions by giving a notice to members of the management bodies and extended a list of transactions that shall not be recognized as related party transactions, including transactions with a value threshold up to which the transactions may be concluded without any corporate measures set forth by Chapter XI of Federal Law No. 208-FZ dated December 26, 1995 'On Joint Stock Companies'.  In the Company's opinion, the formal interest criteria and related cases of abstention from voting on approval of transactions established by the Federal Law 'On Joint Stock Companies' are sufficient to ensure optimal protection of rights and interests of shareholders and investors. Capturing of cases when members of the Board of Directors of the Company and other persons who have no formal interest but who have a conflict of interest or other actual interest shall abstain from voting on approval of a transaction in the internal documents could complicate the Company's operations due to the growing number of related party transactions.  Moreover, it is difficult to define other cases of actual interest in addition to cases set forth by the Federal Law 'On Joint Stock Companies' as at the present time there are no appropriate recommendations and/or clarifications provided by the regulator.  Taking the foregoing into consideration, the Company is not going to make any amendments to the internal documents in the short term.   |
